# Open-status verification

Date checked: 2026-06-09

Target:

> For every connected, locally finite, quasi-transitive, nonamenable graph
> \(G\), prove \(p_c(G)<p_u(G)\) for Bernoulli bond percolation. A stronger
> target is \(p_c(G)<p_{2\to 2}(G)\).

## Current evidence

1. Hutchcroft's 2019 GOFA paper states the Benjamini-Schramm conjecture as:
   for connected locally finite quasi-transitive \(G\), \(p_c(G)<p_u(G)\)
   iff \(G\) is nonamenable. It also notes that the amenable direction is
   known, leaving the nonamenable direction as the hard direction. Source:
   https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00039-019-00498-0

2. The same paper proves the conjecture under the additional assumption of
   Gromov hyperbolicity, not in full generality. It also states the stronger
   conjecture \(p_c(G)<p_{q\to q}(G)\) for all \(q\in(1,\infty)\), equivalent
   to \(p_c(G)<p_{2\to 2}(G)\). Source:
   https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00039-019-00498-0

3. Recent work by Hutchcroft and Pan in 2024 studies percolation at the
   uniqueness threshold and proves non-uniqueness at \(p_u\) for new classes
   of nonamenable groups, including groups with an amenable \(wq\)-normal
   subgroup of exponential growth. This is substantial partial progress, not a
   general solution. Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12283

4. Recent work by Hutchcroft and Pan in 2024 proves sharp uniqueness-threshold
   results for products such as \(T\times \mathbb Z^d\), products of trees with
   amenable Cayley graphs, and lamplighter examples. This again shows active
   partial progress in special classes. Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15895

5. Li's 2026 arXiv preprint treats planar site percolation and a different
   Benjamini-Schramm planar-site conjecture, resolving some regimes and giving
   a counterexample to one planar-site formulation in full generality. This is
   adjacent but does not solve the bond-percolation quasi-transitive
   nonamenable conjecture targeted here. Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09958

6. Terlov and Timar's 2025 preprint on weighted-amenability proves new
   characterizations and a relaxed version of a Hutchcroft conjecture involving
   \(p_h<p_u\) in the weighted/nonunimodular setting. This is further active
   special-case and adjacent progress, not a proof of the full
   \(p_c<p_u\) or \(p_c<p_{2\to2}\) conjecture for all nonamenable
   quasi-transitive graphs. Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.02560

## Verification conclusion

As of the 2026-06-08 search, I found no source announcing a general proof of
\(p_c(G)<p_u(G)\) for every nonamenable quasi-transitive graph, nor of the
stronger \(p_c(G)<p_{2\to 2}(G)\). The current recent literature found is
consistent with the general problem remaining open and with major progress
still occurring by proving important subclasses.

This is therefore a valid target distinct from the locally solved phase
retrieval/RMT problem.

## Continuation refresh

Refreshed on 2026-06-08 during Round 008.  Search results again pointed to
special-case or adjacent work rather than a general solution:

- Hutchcroft's hyperbolic-graph paper remains the source for the \(L^2\)
  formulation and proves the conjecture under hyperbolicity, not generally.
  Source: https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00039-019-00498-0
- Hutchcroft--Pan's subgroup-relativization work treats uniqueness-threshold
  phenomena for classes of nonamenable groups.  Source:
  https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12283
- Hutchcroft--Pan's dimension-jump work treats product and lamplighter
  settings.  Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15895
- Li's 2026 planar site-percolation preprint is adjacent and does not resolve
  the quasi-transitive bond-percolation conjecture targeted here.  Source:
  https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09958

No refreshed source located in this pass announced a proof of the full
nonamenable quasi-transitive bond-percolation conjecture or of the stronger
\(p_c<p_{2\to2}\) statement.

## Continuation refresh, 2026-06-09

The 2026-06-09 refresh again found current literature treating related
questions as active and proving adjacent or special-case results rather than
the full conjecture.

- Hutchcroft's \(L^2\)-boundedness paper remains an explicit source for the
  stronger \(T_{p_c}:L^2\to L^2\) conjecture and for the implication to the
  older non-uniqueness conjecture.  Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.05804
- Terlov--Timar's 2025 weighted-amenability paper proves a relaxed
  weighted/nonunimodular version of a \(p_h<p_u\) conjecture and records this
  as partial progress in a related setting.  Source:
  https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.02560
- A 2025 GOFA paper on sharpness and locality for finite transitive graphs
  describes ongoing active work around Schramm locality and finite analogues of
  infinite transitive-graph percolation, but does not announce a solution of
  the nonamenable \(p_c<p_u\) conjecture targeted here.  Source:
  https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00039-025-00726-w

The selected problem therefore remains a valid open target as of this refresh.

## Continuation update, Round 270, 2026-06-09

Round 270 includes a refreshed external search.  I found no announced general
solution of the Benjamini--Schramm nonuniqueness conjecture for Bernoulli bond
percolation on nonamenable transitive or quasi-transitive graphs, and no
announced general proof of the stronger \(p_c(G)<p_{2\to2}(G)\) target.  The
visible sources remain partial or adjacent:

- Hutchcroft's \(L^2\)-boundedness paper remains the operator-threshold source
  for the stronger \(p_c<p_{2\to2}\) route.  Source:
  https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.05804
- Hutchcroft--Pan's threshold examples are new progress around the same
  threshold landscape, not a resolution of the general conjecture.  Sources:
  https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12283 and https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15895
- Terlov--Timar's weighted-amenability work and Li's 2026 planar-site
  end-structure work remain adjacent or special-setting results, not the
  general Bernoulli bond-percolation theorem targeted here.  Sources:
  https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.02560 and https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09958

Internally, Round 270 tests the most optimistic lower-loss successor route.
Even if the fixed-gap pigeonhole loss were removed, leaving only the count
loss \(K\mapsto2K\), a valid iteration that reaches the slab cutoff would
require
\[
  K\beta\le\frac{\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}}8\ell .
\]
For the raw finite-ladder chain terms this becomes
\[
  512M_iB_iC_i\,\beta_i
  \le
  \frac{\theta_{\lambda_i,\sigma_i,\beta_i}}8q
  \qquad(q=3,4),
\]
which is not supplied by the existing reductions.  The next proof attempt
should therefore focus on improving the initial depth-to-length ratio,
restarting the counted-start supply, or directly estimating the chain-bridge
packets.

## Continuation update, Round 271, 2026-06-09

Round 271 does not include a new external literature search; the external
assessment remains the Round 270 source-checked status.

Internally, the accumulated chain-bridge residuals are simplified.  A
confined staircase-chain bridge with lower-chain length \(\ell\) and gap
\(h\ge\ell+1\) projects to an ordinary confined fixed-gap bridge with gap
\(h-\ell+1\), by taking the last lower-chain run and the first upper-chain
run.  Combining this with multiplicity-free finite chain amplification gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
  P^{\rm chain}_{j,K,L,\ell}
  \le&
  \sum_{a\in I_n(\ell)}P^{\rm chain}_{j,K_n,L,a}
\\
  &+
  \sum_{r=1}^n
  \sum_{g=2}^{B_r-\ell-r+2}
  P^{\rm bridge}_{j,K_r,L,g}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Thus the direct-bridge route is now stated using ordinary confined bridge
profiles only.  The unresolved part is to control those ordinary bridges for
the stage-dependent depth parameters \(K_r\) and gap ranges produced by any
growing-depth chain iteration.

## Continuation update, Round 272, 2026-06-09

Round 272 does not include a new external literature search; the external
assessment remains the Round 270 source-checked status.

Internally, ordinary confined fixed-gap bridge profiles now have an explicit
slab-start cutoff.  Since a bridge of gap \(h\) needs both \(u\) and \(u+h\)
inside the same slab, there are at most \(W_{\beta,p}-h\) possible lower
starts.  Therefore
\[
  P^{\rm bridge}_{j,K,L,h}=0
  \qquad\text{if}\qquad
  W_{\beta,p}-h\le
  \frac{\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}}{4K}L_H,
\]
and in particular all gaps \(h\ge W_{\beta,p}\) vanish.

Combined with the Round 271 chain-bridge projection, this truncates the
finite chain-amplification bridge residuals to slab-scale gaps:
\[
\begin{aligned}
  P^{\rm chain}_{j,K,L,\ell}
  \le&
  \sum_{a\in I_n(\ell)}P^{\rm chain}_{j,K_n,L,a}
\\
  &+
  \sum_{r=1}^n
  \sum_{\substack{2\le g\le B_r-\ell-r+2\\
        g<W_{\beta,p}-\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}L_H/(4K_r)}}
  P^{\rm bridge}_{j,K_r,L,g}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The remaining direct-bridge route is therefore a uniform estimate over
\(O(L_H)\) ordinary bridge gaps at each stage, rather than over the much
larger formal cutoffs \(B_r\).

## Continuation update, Round 273, 2026-06-09

Round 273 does not include a new external literature search; the external
assessment remains the Round 270 source-checked status.

Internally, ordinary confined bridge residuals are split into inward-free
bridges and existing spread-return profiles.  For fixed gap \(h\),
\[
  P^{\rm bridge}_{j,K,L,h}
  \le
  P^{\rm bridge,free}_{j,2K,L,h}
  +
  X^{\rm ret,spr}_{j,\,2K\max\{h-1,1\}}.
\]
The proof is deterministic: if a confined bridge is not inward-free between
its lower and upper record runs, it has an inward token at an intermediate
level, and the existing inward-token lemma gives an exterior return.  The
factor \(\max\{h-1,1\}\) is the maximum number of lower starts that can map
to the same intermediate return level.

After applying the Round 272 gap truncation, finite chain amplification can
now be stated using terminal chain profiles, inward-free bridge profiles, and
spread-return profiles only.  The genuinely new bridge object is therefore
the inward-free confined bridge.

## Continuation update, Round 274, 2026-06-09

Round 274 does not include a new external literature search; the external
assessment remains the Round 270 source-checked status.

Internally, the inward-free bridge residual is removed as a separate object.
An inward-free confined bridge of gap \(h\) is radially nondecreasing between
its lower and upper record runs.  It therefore either forms a locally short
staircase chain of length \(h+1\), or one of its intermediate level runs is
long:
\[
  P^{\rm bridge,free}_{j,K,L,h}
  \le
  P^{\rm chain}_{j,K,L,h+1}
  +
  W_{\beta,p}T^{\rm long}_{j,L}.
\]

Combining this with the Round 273 reduction yields a bridge-free finite chain
amplification: the residuals are terminal chain profiles, additional
slab-scale chain profiles \(P^{\rm chain}_{j,2K_r,L,g+1}\), spread-return
profiles \(X^{\rm ret,spr}_{j,2K_r(g-1)}\), and a long-run term with
exponent-negligible coefficient \(nW_{\beta,p}^2\) for fixed \(n\).  The next
obstruction is therefore uniform control of the p-dependent chain profiles
with lengths up to the slab scale, rather than direct bridge estimates.

## Continuation update, Round 275, 2026-06-09

Round 275 does not include a new external literature search; the external
assessment remains the Round 270 source-checked status.

Internally, the manuscript adds a start-count cutoff for long locally short
staircase chains.  A chain of length \(\ell\) in one heavy slab has at most
\(\max\{W_{\beta,p}-\ell+1,0\}\) possible starts, while the chain profile
requires strictly more than
\(\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}L_H/(4K)\) counted starts.  Hence the chain
profile vanishes if
\[
  W_{\beta,p}-\ell+1\le
  \frac{\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}}{4K}L_H.
\]

Pushing this into the Round 274 bridge-free finite chain amplification
truncates the terminal chain sum to
\[
  a<
  W_{\beta,p}+1-\frac{\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}}{4K_n}L_H.
\]
The bridge-derived chain and spread-return residuals remain restricted by the
existing slab-truncated \(g\)-range.  This sharpens the finite reduction but
does not by itself prove the target inequality; the remaining issue is still
uniform control of slab-scale chain profiles and the existing return terms.

## Continuation update, Round 276, 2026-06-09

Round 276 includes a refreshed external search.  I found no announced general

nonamenable quasi-transitive graphs, and no announced proof of the stronger

- Hutchcroft's \(L^2\)-boundedness paper remains the operator-threshold
  source for \(p_c<p_{2\to2}\) as a sufficient condition:
  https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.05804
- The arXiv searches surfaced recent percolation work in special or adjacent

  https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15895, https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.02560, and

Internally, the successor-gap count is sharpened by using the residual
chain-start width
\[
  R_{\ell,p}=\max\{W_{\beta,p}-\ell+1,0\}.
\]
This gives a residual cutoff
\[
  B^{\rm res}_{K,\ell,p}
  =
  \left\lceil
    \frac{16K R_{\ell,p}}{\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}L_H}
  \right\rceil+1
\]
instead of the crude slab-width cutoff.  In the near-top regime
\(R_{\ell,p}\le C L_H/K\), the successor cutoff is bounded independently of
\(p\).  This identifies a cheaper amplification regime near slab exhaustion,
but the manuscript still lacks a mechanism that brings fixed initial chain
lengths into that regime without the old growing \(K\)-loss.

## Continuation update, Round 277, 2026-06-09

Round 277 does not include a new external literature search; the external
assessment remains the Round 276 source-checked status.

Internally, the residual-width successor cutoff is pushed through the
existing one-step chain-amplification mechanism.  The manuscript now has a
residual-width one-step inequality with
\[
  K^{\rm res}_{K,\ell,p}=2K B^{\rm res}_{K,\ell,p},
\]
where \(B^{\rm res}_{K,\ell,p}\) depends on the remaining chain-start width

profiles and large gaps into confined staircase-chain bridge profiles, exactly

The near-top specialization gives a p-independent cost:
if \(R_{\ell,p}\le C L_H/K\), then the one-step reduction uses the fixed

tool near slab exhaustion, but it still does not prove the target theorem;
the missing ingredient remains a route from fixed initial chain lengths into
that near-top regime without losing the counting range.

## Continuation update, Round 278, 2026-06-09

Round 278 does not include a new external literature search; the external
assessment remains the Round 276 source-checked status.

Internally, the manuscript now isolates a bridge-free residual descent
regime.  If the residual cutoff satisfies
\[
  B^{\rm res}_{K,\ell,p}\le \ell,
\]
then the residual-width one-step amplification produces only longer chain
profiles and no confined staircase-chain bridge profiles.  Moreover, each
longer chain term decreases the residual start width:
\[
  R_{\ell+h,p}=\max\{R_{\ell,p}-h,0\}\le\max\{R_{\ell,p}-1,0\}.
\]
A sufficient condition is
\[
  R_{\ell,p}\le
  \frac{\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}(\ell-1)}{16K}L_H .
\]
This is a clean local descent mechanism near slab exhaustion, but it still
does not prove the target theorem because the manuscript does not yet supply
a path from fixed initial chain lengths into that bridge-free near-top
regime.

## Continuation update, Round 279, 2026-06-09

Round 279 does not include a new external literature search; the external
assessment remains the Round 276 source-checked status.

Internally, the bridge-free residual descent is iterated conditionally.  The
manuscript now defines a finite descent tree with node parameters
\(K(\mathbf h),\ell(\mathbf h)\) and cutoff
\[
  B(\mathbf h)=B^{\rm res}_{K(\mathbf h),\ell(\mathbf h),p}.
\]
If every node up to depth \(m-1\) stays in the counting range and satisfies
\(B(\mathbf h)\le\ell(\mathbf h)\), then the initial chain profile is bounded
by the terminal chain profiles at depth \(m\), with no bridge residuals.  The
terminal residual start width is at most
\[
  \max\{R_{\ell,p}-m,0\}.
\]
Thus, if the hypotheses persisted through \(m\ge R_{\ell,p}\), the initial
chain profile would vanish by the start-count cutoff.  This is still
conditional: the proof does not yet show that the hypotheses persist from the
fixed small chain lengths produced earlier in the reduction.

## Continuation update, Round 280, 2026-06-09

Round 280 does not include a new external literature search; the external
assessment remains the Round 276 source-checked status.

Internally, the manuscript now proves that the conditional bridge-free
descent iteration has a logarithmic depth obstruction.  If its hypotheses
persist until the initial residual width \(R_{\ell,p}\) is exhausted, then
\[
  4^{R_{\ell,p}-1}K
  <
  \frac{\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}}{16}L_H,
\]
or equivalently
\[
  R_{\ell,p}
  <
  1+
  \log_4\!\left(
    \frac{\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}L_H}{16K}
  \right).
\]
Thus the current bridge-free descent mechanism can certify exhaustion only
from logarithmic residual width.  The remaining gap is to enter this regime
with controlled \(K\), or to replace the current depth-parameter growth.

## Continuation update, Round 281, 2026-06-09

Round 281 does not include a new external literature search; the external
assessment remains the Round 276 source-checked status.

Internally, the manuscript now converts the logarithmic obstruction into a
first-obstruction alternative along the unit residual-descent branch.  If
\[
  4^{R_{\ell,p}-1}K
  \ge
  \frac{\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}}{16}L_H,
\]
then before residual exhaustion the unit branch must hit a first index where
either the counted-start range saturates,
\[
  K_r\ge
  \frac{\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}}{16}L_H,
\]
or the residual cutoff is bridge-active,
\[
  B^{\rm res}_{K_r,\ell+r,p}>\ell+r.
\]
In the bridge-active case, the residual-width one-step amplification contains
a non-empty confined chain-bridge sum.  This does not solve the target
theorem, but it identifies the concrete alternatives that any continuation
of the near-top descent program must control.

## Continuation update, Round 282, 2026-06-09

Round 282 does not include a new external literature search; the external
assessment remains the Round 276 source-checked status.

Internally, the manuscript now refines the bridge-active side of the

projects to ordinary confined fixed-gap bridge profiles:
\[
\begin{aligned}
  P^{\rm chain}_{j,K_{r},L,\ell_r}
  \le&
  \sum_{h=1}^{\ell_r}
  P^{\rm chain}_{j,K_{r+1},L,\ell_r+h}
\\
  &+
  \sum_{g=2}^{B_r-\ell_r+1}
  P^{\rm bridge}_{j,K_{r+1},L,g}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The bridge-active condition also forces

  R_{\ell_r,p}>
  \frac{\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}(\ell_r-1)}
       {16K_r}L_H.
\]
Thus this branch of the obstruction is now expressed using the ordinary
bridge profiles already appearing elsewhere in the reduction.  The target
theorem remains open in the manuscript.

## Continuation update, Round 286, 2026-06-09

Round 286 does not include a new external literature search; the external

## Continuation update, Round 287, 2026-06-09

Round 287 does not include a new external literature search; the external

\[

\]
so the bridge-free child sum has at most \(W_{\beta,p}\) terms, with

Thus uniform exponent control over the child profiles passes to the parent

## Continuation update, Round 288, 2026-06-09

Round 288 does not include a new external literature search; the external

Internally, the manuscript now refines the large-window branch after counting

\[
  K_{r+1}\ge
  \frac{\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}}{16}L_H
\]
in that branch, each child ordinary chain profile has start threshold at most

The target theorem remains open; the remaining large-window question is now

## Continuation update, Round 289, 2026-06-09

Round 289 does not include a new external literature search; the external
assessment remains the Round 285 source-checked status.

Internally, the manuscript now localizes finite-start chain profiles to

start \(u\), and there are at most \(W_{\beta,p}\) possible starts in the
slab, so it is bounded by \(W_{\beta,p}\) times the corresponding exact-start
profile.  In the large-window branch this gives
\[
  P^{\rm chain}_{j,K_r,L,\ell_r}
  \le
  5W_{\beta,p}
  \sum_{h=1}^{B_r}
  P^{\rm chain,exact}_{j,K_{r+1},L,\ell_r+h},
\]
with total expanded cost at most \(5W_{\beta,p}^2\), still subexponential on
the \(L_H\) scale.

The target theorem remains open; the large-window branch is now reduced to
uniform control of exact-start chain profiles over a subexponential child
family.

## Continuation update, Round 290, 2026-06-09

Round 290 does not include a new external literature search; the external
assessment remains the Round 285 source-checked status.

Internally, the manuscript strengthens the exact-start localization cost.
For a chain of length \(\ell\), there are at most
\[
  R_{\ell,p}=\max\{W_{\beta,p}-\ell+1,0\}
\]
admissible starts in a slab, not \(W_{\beta,p}\).  Thus few-start profiles

\[

  \le

\]
where \(R_{\ell_r+h,p}=\max\{R_{\ell_r,p}-h,0\}\).  The total cost is still
subexponential, but now tracks the residual-width descent across the child
family.

The target theorem remains open; the remaining problem is to obtain uniform
exact-start chain bounds that exploit this residual weighting.

## Continuation update, Round 293, 2026-06-09

Round 293 does not include a new external literature search; the external
assessment remains the Round 285 source-checked status.

Internally, the manuscript now adds a direct deterministic reduction for
exact-start chains.  If an exact-start chain has length
\[
  \ell>
  \frac{\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}}{4K}L_H ,
\]
then its \(\ell\) consecutive crossing levels already witness the existing
prefix-record outward-spread profile.  In the large-window branch the child
parameter satisfies
\[
  K_{r+1}\ge \frac{\theta_{\lambda,\sigma,\beta}}{16}L_H ,
\]
so every exact-start child of total length at least \(5\) is absorbed by

Consequently, with
\[
  \mathcal S_r=\{h\in\{1,\ldots,B_r\}:\ell_r+h\le4\},
\]
the large-window exact-start branch is bounded by the existing
prefix-record outward-spread profile plus at most two short exact-start
children.  The prefix-record coefficient is at most \(5W_{\beta,p}^2\), and
the short-child coefficient is at most \(10W_{\beta,p}\), both
subexponential.

The target theorem remains open; the remaining new exact-start obstruction
has been narrowed to total chain lengths \(3\) and \(4\).

## Continuation update, Round 295, 2026-06-09

Round 295 does not include a new external literature search; the external

\[

  \qquad q_{r,p}=\lfloor A_r\rfloor+1 .
\]
Any \(q_{r,p}\)-start child of length larger than \(A_r\) is already a

\[

\]
the large-window branch is bounded by

\\

\]

For each exceptional child,

\]
Thus the only remaining large-window novelty is a finite surplus-start
obstruction of type \((3,4)\), \((3,5)\), or \((4,5)\).  The record-spread
coefficient is now only \(B_r<W_{\beta,p}\), improving on the
residual-localized \(O(W_{\beta,p}^2)\) exact-start coefficient.

The target theorem remains open; the next local issue is to exploit the
surplus-start condition \(q>\ell\) in those three finite exception types.

## Continuation update, Round 339, 2026-06-10

Round 339 includes a fresh targeted external status refresh.  I located no
announcement or paper proving the general Benjamini--Schramm non-uniqueness
conjecture for all nonamenable quasi-transitive graphs.  The located sources
remain consistent with the same status:

- Hutchcroft's \(L^2\)-boundedness paper still treats
  \(p_c(G)<p_{2\to2}(G)\) as a conjectural sufficient route rather than a
  proved theorem in the general quasi-transitive case:
  https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.05804
- Hermon--Hutchcroft prove the conjecture in the nonunimodular case, a known
  special case rather than the general quasi-transitive target:
  https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00222-020-01011-3
- Recent planar site-percolation sources concern a different planar/site
  setting and do not settle the general quasi-transitive bond-percolation
  target:
  https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/forum-of-mathematics-sigma/article/benjaminischramm-conjecture-for-planar-site-percolation/6A9451491020616A7C2B7D03B0F645C8
  and https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09958

Internally, the manuscript now splits the confined
bridge-record-record-record-record-record-record fixed-gap bridge family into
an inward-free bridge family plus a bridge-return spread family:
\[

  \le
  P^{\rm pre,br,free,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,ch}_{i,j,h}
  +
  R^{\rm spr,br,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,ch}_{i,j,h}.
\]
Here
\[
  P^{\rm pre,br,free,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,ch}_{i,j,h}
  =
  P^{\rm pre,out,per,bridge,free}_{j,
    2K_i^{\rm gap,br,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,ch},L_i,h}
\]
and
\[
  R^{\rm spr,br,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,ch}_{i,j,h}
  =
  X^{\rm rad,ret,spr}_{j,
    2K_i^{\rm gap,br,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,ch}\max\{h-1,1\}}.
\]
The finite-ladder, radial-tail, and subdivision criteria now replace the
confined bridge hypothesis by these two hypotheses under the same coefficient
\(D_i^{\rm rec,br,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,ch}\).  The adjacent return-spread
family \(R^{\rm spr,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,recbr,ch}\), all earlier
bridge-return and return-spread families, and the long-run coefficient

## Targeted external status check after Round 382, 2026-06-10

I ran a targeted current-source check around the general Benjamini--Schramm
non-uniqueness conjecture and the stronger \(L^2\) target
\(\pc(G)<p_{2\to2}(G)\).  The located primary sources still support treating
the project target as open in full generality.

- Hutchcroft's \(L^2\)-boundedness paper states the nonamenable
  \(L^2\)-boundedness condition as a conjecture, proves consequences of
  \(\pc<p_{2\to2}\), and verifies the condition only in several classes.
  Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.05804
- Hutchcroft--Pan's subgroup-relativization paper, arXiv:2409.12283v1,
  states that the Benjamini--Schramm middle-phase conjecture has been proved
  for several classes but remains open in general.  It adds new examples and
  relative tools at \(p_u\), rather than a general proof of
  \(\pc<\pu\) or \(\pc<p_{2\to2}\).
  Source: https://arxiv.org/html/2409.12283v1
- Hutchcroft--Pan's dimension-jump paper proves, for products of regular
  trees with infinite amenable Cayley graphs and for lamplighter examples,
  that several thresholds including \(p_{2\to2}\) coincide with \(p_u\).
  This is special-case progress, not a general theorem for all nonamenable
  transitive or quasi-transitive graphs.
  Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15895
- Li's 2026 planar site-percolation papers prove or discuss planar/site
  variants and properly embedded planar cases.  These do not prove the
  general Bernoulli bond-percolation Benjamini--Schramm theorem or the
  stronger \(L^2\) operator threshold target used in this project.
  Sources: https://arxiv.org/html/2304.00923v6 and
  https://arxiv.org/html/2601.09958v2

## Continuation update, Round 399, 2026-06-10

Round 399 includes a targeted external status refresh.  Searches and source
checks found no current announcement of a general proof of the
Benjamini--Schramm nonamenable quasi-transitive bond-percolation conjecture
\(p_c(G)<p_u(G)\), nor of the stronger \(p_c(G)<p_{2\to2}(G)\) target.  The
current sources checked still point to the general problem as open or to
special-case and adjacent progress:

- Hutchcroft's hyperbolic-graph paper states the conjecture and proves it
  under Gromov hyperbolicity, not in full generality.  Source:
  https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00039-019-00498-0
- Hutchcroft's \(L^2\)-boundedness preprint remains the operator-threshold
  source for the stronger \(p_c<p_{2\to2}\) route.  Source:
  https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.05804
- Hutchcroft--Pan's recent work treats uniqueness-threshold phenomena in
  special classes of nonamenable groups and products, not the full
  quasi-transitive bond-percolation conjecture.  Sources:
  https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.12283 and
  https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.15895
- Terlov--Timar's weighted-amenability work is adjacent weighted or
  nonunimodular progress rather than a general proof for all nonamenable

## Continuation update, Round 421, 2026-07-06

non-uniqueness conjecture and the stronger \(p_c<p_{2\to2}\) target.  The
located primary and near-primary sources still support treating the full
nonamenable quasi-transitive target as open.  Hutchcroft's \(L^2\)-boundedness
paper states the \(L^2\) boundedness condition as a conjectural nonamenable
percolation input and develops its consequences rather than proving it in full
generality.  A 2026 planar-site preprint concerns a planar/end-structure
variant and does not resolve the general bond-percolation target used here.
Hutchcroft's recent survey-style discussion still lists the
Benjamini-Schramm conjecture as a prominent open problem for nonamenable
Cayley graphs.

from Round 420 into a finite fixed-gap family:
